`file_key` is cleared only after both encryption and ECIES wrapping succeed. If either call returns `Err`, the raw file key is dropped without `clear_bytes`, despite the comment saying fallible paths are covered.

-        let wrapped_key = cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(&file_key, &self.public_key)
-            .map_err(|e| format!("Key wrapping failed: {}", e))?;
+        let ciphertext = match cipherbox_crypto::aes::encrypt_aes_gcm(&plaintext, &file_key, &iv) {
+            Ok(ciphertext) => ciphertext,
+            Err(e) => {
+                cipherbox_crypto::utils::clear_bytes(&mut file_key);
+                return Err(format!("File encryption failed: {}", e));
+            }
+        };
+
+        let wrapped_key = match cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(&file_key, &self.public_key) {

+                cipherbox_crypto::utils::clear_bytes(&mut file_key);
+                return Err(format!("Key wrapping failed: {}", e));
+            }

=== comment id 3410495615
path: crates/fuse/src/lib.rs
line: None start_line: None orig: 222
subject: line
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Classify replay IPNS resolution without cache fallback.**

`resolve_ipns_for_replay` calls `PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence`, which returns a cached sequence on resolve failure. That can turn a transient/non-404 resolve failure into `Found(cached)` and allow replay to continue instead of retaining the journal entry through the typed `Error` branch. Use a strict resolve path here, or add a strict coordinator method, so only a real IPNS resolve success becomes `Found`.

behavior that masks transient or non-404 errors by returning cached sequences,
causing the Error branch to be skipped and allowing replay to continue
incorrectly. To fix this, either call a strict resolve method on the coordinator
that bypasses the cache fallback behavior (if one exists), or create a new
strict coordinator method that performs IPNS resolution without cache fallback.

**Do not publish an empty `FilePointer` as a successful replay.**

When `file_meta_ipns_name` is `None`, Step 3 skips per-file metadata publish, but Step 4 publishes a parent `FilePointer` whose locator is `""`; `replay_for_vault` can then remove the journal. The uploaded CID has no resolvable file metadata record, and multiple legacy empty-name entries also collide under `merge_folder_children`’ IPNS-key merge semantics. Return an error/park the entry unless replay can create a real IPNS name/key for this legacy case.

Author: Michael Yankelev <myankelev@gmail.com>

    docs: capture todos for deferred replay hardening from PR 491 review

> AGENT

Both outside-of-diff comments are **valid**. Here's my assessment:

## Comment 1 — mkdir inode rollback on journal failure (Major, but low-impact)

**Verdict: valid, cheap fix, do it now.**

In both `write_ops.rs:518-546` and `platform/windows/write_ops.rs:126-153`, the inode is inserted and the parent's `children`/mtime mutated *before* `build_mkdir_journal_entry?` and `journal.put?`. If either `?` returns `Err`, the closure errors back to the OS but leaves a ghost dir in `fs.inodes` with no durable replay record.

Caveat CodeRabbit understates: the inode table is **ephemeral** — rebuilt from IPNS on remount — so this is a transient in-session inconsistency, not data loss. Trigger is rare (crypto build failure or fsync IO error). But the fix is the standard insert-then-rollback discipline, ~8 lines × 2 sites, clearly correct. Worth taking.

## Comment 2 — replay_mkdir_entry never republishes the child folder's seq-0 IPNS (Critical, heavier)

**Verdict: valid, and it's a real asymmetry in this PR's own durability work.**

`replay_mkdir_entry` (lib.rs:1607) only calls `fetch_merge_publish_parent` — it adds the child `FolderEntry` to the parent but never recreates the child's initial empty `FolderMetadata` / seq-0 IPNS record. Compare `replay_upload_entry`, which *was* hardened (Step 3, #19/#20/F3) to idempotently re-publish per-file IPNS with first-publish detection + TEE enrollment.

So: crash after `journal.put` but before the background thread published the child IPNS → replay merges a parent pointing at a child IPNS name that resolves to nothing. Navigating into the folder breaks. That defeats the crash-recovery guarantee the PR is establishing. The fix mirrors the file path: unwrap child folder key + child IPNS key, build empty `FolderMetadata`, first-publish-detect via `resolve_ipns_for_replay`, publish seq-0, then merge parent.

## Note on the already-deferred TODOs

The two TODOs committed in `a84fdf4` cover the *inline* Major comments (cache-fallback resolve, empty-`FilePointer`), **not** these two outside-diff comments. So neither of these is logged yet.

## Recommendation

- **Comment 1**: implement now (small, clearly correct).
- **Comment 2**: it's the same class as the deferred items but arguably a genuine gap in the *new* mkdir durability path. Either fix it in this PR for symmetry with the file path, or log a third follow-up TODO matching the existing two.

Want me to implement #1 and log #2 as a TODO, or implement both?

635	                    let parent_key_arr: [u8; 32] = parent_ipns_key.try_into()
636	                        .map_err(|_| "Invalid parent IPNS key length".to_string())?;
637	                    let new_seq = seq + 1;
638	                    let parent_value = format!("/ipfs/{}", parent_meta_cid);
639	                    let parent_record = cipherbox_core::ipns::create_ipns_record(
640	                        &parent_key_arr, &parent_value, new_seq, 86_400_000,
641	                    ).map_err(|e| format!("Parent IPNS record failed: {}", e))?;
642	                    let parent_marshaled = cipherbox_core::ipns::marshal_ipns_record(
643	                        &parent_record,
644	                    ).map_err(|e| format!("Parent IPNS marshal failed: {}", e))?;
645	                    let parent_record_b64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD
646	                        .encode(&parent_marshaled);
647	
648	                    // Parent folder publish after mkdir includes conflict detection.
649	                    // On conflict, signal the FS thread via upload_tx so the debounced
650	                    // publisher retries with a fresh sequence (D-11a).
651	                    let parent_req = cipherbox_api_client::IpnsPublishRequest {
652	                        ipns_name: parent_ipns_name.clone(),
653	                        record: parent_record_b64,
654	                        metadata_cid: parent_meta_cid,
655	                        encrypted_ipns_private_key: None,
656	                        key_epoch: None,
657	                        expected_sequence_number: Some(seq.to_string()),
658	                    };
659	                    match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::publish_ipns(&api, &parent_req).await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))? {
660	                        cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Success => {
661	                            coordinator.record_publish(&parent_ipns_name, new_seq);
662	                            // Only unpin old CID on successful publish
663	                            if let Some(old) = parent_old_cid {
664	                                let _ = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::unpin_content(&api, &old).await;
665	                            }
666	                            // Remove journal entry now that parent publish is confirmed (D-11b).
667	                            let _ = journal_for_mkdir.remove(&mkdir_journal_entry_id);
668	                            log::info!("Parent metadata published after mkdir");
669	                        }
670	                        cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Conflict { current_sequence_number } => {
671	                            // Signal the FS thread to re-arm the debounced publisher with a
672	                            // fresh sequence (D-11a). Journal entry stays until parent publish
673	                            // confirms (D-11b) — do NOT remove it here.
674	                            log::warn!(
675	                                "Conflict on parent mkdir publish (expected seq {}, server has {}). Signalling retry.",
676	                                seq, current_sequence_number
677	                            );
678	                            let _ = upload_tx.send(crate::FsEvent::MkdirConflict { parent_ino });
679	                        }
680	                    }
681	                    Ok::<(), String>(())
682	                });
683	
684	                if let Err(e) = result {
685	                    log::error!("Background mkdir publish failed: {}", e);
686	                }
687	            });
688	
689	            Ok(fuse_attr)
690	        })();
691	
692	        match result {
693	            Ok(fuse_attr) => {
694	                reply.entry(&DIR_TTL, &fuse_attr, 0);
695	            }
696	            Err(e) => {
697	                log::error!("mkdir failed: {}", e);
698	                reply.error(libc::EIO);

125	    pub async fn publish_file_metadata(
126	        api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
127	        file_meta: &cipherbox_core::FileMetadata,
128	        folder_key: &[u8],
129	        file_ipns_private_key: &zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
130	        file_ipns_name: &str,
131	        coordinator: &crate::PublishCoordinator,
132	        tee_public_key: Option<&[u8]>,
133	        tee_key_epoch: Option<u32>,
134	        is_first_publish: bool,
135	    ) -> Result<(), String> {
136	        let folder_key_arr: [u8; 32] = folder_key.try_into()
137	            .map_err(|_| "Invalid folder key length for FileMetadata encryption".to_string())?;
138	
139	        let sealed = cipherbox_core::folder::encrypt_file_metadata(file_meta, &folder_key_arr)
140	            .map_err(|e| format!("FileMetadata encryption failed: {}", e))?;
141	
142	        let iv_hex = hex::encode(&sealed[..12]);
143	        use base64::Engine;
144	        let data_base64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&sealed[12..]);
145	        let json = serde_json::json!({ "iv": iv_hex, "data": data_base64 });
146	        let json_bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&json)
147	            .map_err(|e| format!("FileMetadata JSON serialization failed: {}", e))?;
148	
149	        let file_meta_cid = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::upload_content(api, &json_bytes)
150	            .await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))?;
151	
152	        let current_seq = if is_first_publish {
153	            None
154	        } else {
155	            Some(coordinator.resolve_sequence(api, file_ipns_name).await?)
156	        };
157	
158	        let ipns_key_arr: [u8; 32] = file_ipns_private_key.as_slice().try_into()
159	            .map_err(|_| "Invalid file IPNS private key length".to_string())?;
160	        let new_seq = crate::next_file_publish_sequence(is_first_publish, current_seq)?;
161	        let value = format!("/ipfs/{}", file_meta_cid);
162	        let record = cipherbox_core::create_ipns_record(&ipns_key_arr, &value, new_seq, 86_400_000)
163	            .map_err(|e| format!("File IPNS record creation failed: {}", e))?;
164	        let marshaled = cipherbox_core::marshal_ipns_record(&record)
165	            .map_err(|e| format!("File IPNS record marshal failed: {}", e))?;
166	
167	        let record_b64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&marshaled);
168	
169	        // TEE enrollment on first publish only (same pattern as folder creation in write_ops.rs)
170	        let (encrypted_ipns_for_tee, tee_epoch) = match (is_first_publish, tee_public_key, tee_key_epoch) {
171	            (true, Some(tee_key), Some(epoch)) => {
172	                let wrapped = cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(
173	                    file_ipns_private_key.as_slice(), tee_key
174	                ).map_err(|e| format!("TEE key wrapping failed: {}", e))?;
175	                (Some(hex::encode(&wrapped)), Some(epoch))
176	            }
177	            (true, Some(_), None) => {
178	                return Err("TEE public key present but key_epoch missing".to_string());
179	            }
180	            _ => (None, None),
181	        };
182	
183	        let req = cipherbox_api_client::IpnsPublishRequest {
184	            ipns_name: file_ipns_name.to_string(),
185	            record: record_b64,
186	            metadata_cid: file_meta_cid.clone(),
187	            encrypted_ipns_private_key: encrypted_ipns_for_tee,
188	            key_epoch: tee_epoch,
189	            expected_sequence_number: None,
190	        };
191	        match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::publish_ipns(api, &req).await.map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))? {
192	            cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Success => {}
193	            cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Conflict { .. } => {
194	                log::warn!("Unexpected conflict on per-file IPNS publish for {}", file_ipns_name);
195	            }
196	        }
197	
198	        coordinator.record_publish(file_ipns_name, new_seq);
199	        log::info!("Per-file IPNS publish succeeded for {}", file_ipns_name);
200	        Ok(())
201	    }

378	    pub fn build_mkdir_journal_entry(
379	        &self,
380	        parent_ino: u64,
381	        child_ino: u64,
382	        name: &str,
383	        folder_key: &[u8],
384	        ipns_name: &str,
385	        ipns_private_key: zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
386	        encrypted_folder_key_hex: &str,
387	    ) -> Result<MkdirJournalResult, String> {
388	        let metadata = cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata {
389	            version: "v2".to_string(),
390	            children: vec![],
391	        };
392	
393	        let json_bytes = crate::encrypt_metadata_to_json(&metadata, folder_key)?;
394	
395	        // TEE-wrap the child IPNS private key for republishing (if TEE configured).
396	        let encrypted_ipns_for_tee = if let Some(ref tee_key) = self.tee_public_key {
397	            let wrapped = cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, tee_key)
398	                .map_err(|e| format!("TEE key wrapping failed: {}", e))?;
399	            Some(hex::encode(&wrapped))
400	        } else {
401	            None
402	        };
403	        let tee_key_epoch = self.tee_key_epoch;
404	
405	        let (parent_metadata, parent_folder_key, parent_ipns_key, parent_ipns_name, parent_old_cid) =
406	            self.build_folder_metadata(parent_ino)?;
407	
408	        let mkdir_created_at_ms = current_unix_ms();
409	
410	        // CR-01: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key for replay signing.
411	        // An empty string (on wrap failure) parks the entry on replay rather than
412	        // persisting a degraded key silently.
413	        let parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal =
414	            wrap_key_to_hex(&parent_ipns_key, &self.public_key, "parent IPNS key");
415	
416	        // CR-03: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key (NOT TEE-wrapped) —
417	        // replay writes this into FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted, which must
418	        // be user-ECIES-wrapped.  An empty string parks the entry rather than
419	        // bricking the folder with an unusable key.
420	        let child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped =
421	            wrap_key_to_hex(&ipns_private_key, &self.public_key, "child IPNS key");
422	
423	        let entry = cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry {
424	            id: generate_entry_id(),
425	            vault_root_ipns: self.root_ipns_name.clone(),
426	            op: cipherbox_sdk::JournalOp::MkdirPublish {
427	                child_ipns_name: ipns_name.to_string(),
428	                child_folder_key_hex: encrypted_folder_key_hex.to_string(),
429	                child_ipns_key_hex: child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped,
430	                parent_folder_ipns_name: parent_ipns_name.clone(),
431	                parent_ipns_key_hex: parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal,
432	                name: name.to_string(),
433	                created_at_ms: mkdir_created_at_ms,
434	            },
435	            retries: 0,
436	            status: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntryStatus::Pending,
437	        };
438	
439	        Ok(MkdirJournalResult {
440	            entry,
441	            json_bytes,
442	            ipns_private_key,
443	            encrypted_ipns_for_tee,
444	            tee_key_epoch,
445	            ipns_name: ipns_name.to_string(),
446	            parent_metadata,
447	            parent_folder_key,
448	            parent_ipns_key,
449	            parent_ipns_name,
450	            parent_old_cid,
451	            ino: child_ino,
452	        })

1338	                                entry.id, filename, journal.max_retries, e
1339	                            ),
1340	                            Ok(_) => log::warn!(

201	/// Classifies an IPNS resolve result into a typed [`crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome`].
202	///
203	/// Wraps [`PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence`] and maps:
204	/// - `Ok(seq)` → `Found(seq)`
205	/// - `Err(e)` where `e` signals 404 / "not found" → `NotFound`
206	/// - `Err(e)` otherwise → `Error(e)` (non-404; entry should be retained)
207	///
208	/// Centralises the brittle substring match (#19) so `replay_upload_entry` matches
209	/// on the typed enum rather than calling `.to_lowercase().contains("not found")` inline.
210	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
211	async fn resolve_ipns_for_replay(
212	    coordinator: &PublishCoordinator,
213	    api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
214	    ipns_name: &str,
215	) -> crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome {
216	    classify_resolve_outcome(coordinator.resolve_sequence(api, ipns_name).await)
217	}
218	
219	/// Pure classification of a `resolve_sequence` result into an [`crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome`].
220	///
221	/// Split out from [`resolve_ipns_for_replay`] so the brittle "not found" / "404"
222	/// substring contract (#19) is unit-testable without a live API client:
223	/// - `Ok(seq)` → `Found(seq)`
224	/// - `Err(e)` whose text signals 404 / "not found" → `NotFound`
225	/// - any other `Err(e)` → `Error(e)` (entry retained on replay)
226	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
227	fn classify_resolve_outcome(result: Result<u64, String>) -> crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome {
228	    use crate::error::IpnsResolveOutcome;
229	    match result {
230	        Ok(seq) => IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(seq),
231	        Err(e) if e.to_lowercase().contains("not found") || e.contains("404") => {
232	            IpnsResolveOutcome::NotFound
233	        }
234	        Err(e) => IpnsResolveOutcome::Error(e),
235	    }
236	}

238	/// Coordinates IPNS publish operations to prevent sequence number races.
239	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
240	pub struct PublishCoordinator {
241	    seq_cache: std::sync::Mutex<HashMap<String, u64>>,
242	    publish_locks: std::sync::Mutex<HashMap<String, Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<()>>>>,
243	}
244	
245	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
246	impl PublishCoordinator {
247	    pub fn new() -> Self {
248	        Self {
249	            seq_cache: std::sync::Mutex::new(HashMap::new()),
250	            publish_locks: std::sync::Mutex::new(HashMap::new()),
251	        }
252	    }
253	
254	    pub fn get_lock(&self, ipns_name: &str) -> Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<()>> {
255	        let mut locks = self.publish_locks.lock().unwrap();
256	        locks
257	            .entry(ipns_name.to_string())
258	            .or_insert_with(|| Arc::new(tokio::sync::Mutex::new(())))
259	            .clone()
260	    }
261	
262	    pub async fn resolve_sequence(
263	        &self,
264	        api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
265	        ipns_name: &str,
266	    ) -> Result<u64, String> {
267	        match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(api, ipns_name).await {
268	            Ok(resp) => {
269	                let resolved = resp.sequence_number.parse::<u64>().unwrap_or_else(|e| {
270	                    log::warn!(
271	                        "Failed to parse IPNS sequence '{}' for {}: {}",
272	                        resp.sequence_number,
273	                        ipns_name,
274	                        e
275	                    );
276	                    0
277	                });
278	                let cached = self.get_cached(ipns_name).unwrap_or(0);
279	                let seq = std::cmp::max(resolved, cached);
280	                self.update_cache(ipns_name, seq);
281	                Ok(seq)
282	            }
283	            Err(e) => match self.get_cached(ipns_name) {
284	                Some(cached) => {
285	                    log::warn!(
286	                        "IPNS resolve failed for {}, using cached seq {}: {}",
287	                        ipns_name,
288	                        cached,
289	                        e
290	                    );
291	                    Ok(cached)
292	                }
293	                None => Err(format!(
294	                    "IPNS resolve failed and no cached sequence for {}: {}",
295	                    ipns_name, e
296	                )),
297	            },
298	        }
299	    }
300	
301	    pub fn record_publish(&self, ipns_name: &str, published_seq: u64) {
302	        self.update_cache(ipns_name, published_seq);
303	    }
304	
305	    fn get_cached(&self, ipns_name: &str) -> Option<u64> {
306	        self.seq_cache.lock().unwrap().get(ipns_name).copied()
307	    }

320	pub fn encrypt_metadata_to_json(
321	    metadata: &cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
322	    folder_key: &[u8],
323	) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
324	    let folder_key_arr: [u8; 32] = folder_key
325	        .try_into()
326	        .map_err(|_| "Invalid folder key length".to_string())?;
327	    let sealed = cipherbox_core::folder::encrypt_folder_metadata(metadata, &folder_key_arr)
328	        .map_err(|e| format!("Metadata encryption failed: {}", e))?;
329	    let iv_hex = hex::encode(&sealed[..12]);
330	    use base64::Engine;
331	    let data_base64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&sealed[12..]);
332	    let json = serde_json::json!({ "iv": iv_hex, "data": data_base64 });
333	    serde_json::to_vec(&json).map_err(|e| format!("JSON serialization failed: {}", e))
334	}
335	
336	/// Merge local children onto remote children to resolve a concurrent-edit conflict.
337	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
338	pub fn merge_folder_children(
339	    local: &cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
340	    remote: cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
341	) -> cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata {
342	    use cipherbox_core::folder::FolderChild;
343	
344	    fn child_ipns_key(child: &FolderChild) -> &str {
345	        match child {
346	            FolderChild::Folder(f) => f.ipns_name.as_str(),
347	            FolderChild::File(f) => f.file_meta_ipns_name.as_str(),
348	        }
349	    }

> AGENT

Both fixes are implemented and verified. The `winfsp` errors above are just the Windows-only `winfsp-sys`/`windows_core` deps failing to build on macOS — unrelated to the change; CI validates that path on Windows.

## Summary

**#1 — mkdir inode rollback (both platforms)**
- `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs:532` and `crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:139`: converted the `build_mkdir_journal_entry?` and `journal.put?` calls into explicit error arms that call `fs.inodes.remove(ino)` before returning. `InodeTable::remove` already cleans the inode, the `name_to_ino` index, and the parent's `children` entry — so a failed journal build/fsync no longer strands a ghost directory with no durable replay record.

**#2 — replay child folder seq-0 IPNS publish**
- Added `publish_child_folder_metadata` helper (lib.rs) mirroring `publish_file_metadata`: encrypts the empty `{version:"v2",children:[]}`, uploads, creates a seq-0 IPNS record, TEE-enrolls the child key on first publish, and publishes.
- `replay_mkdir_entry` now re-publishes the child record **before** merging the parent, idempotently: `resolve_ipns_for_replay(child_ipns_name)` → `Found` skips (already published pre-crash), `NotFound` unwraps the child folder + IPNS keys and publishes seq-0, `Error` retains the entry. Added an empty-`child_ipns_key_hex` guard matching the existing parent-key guard.
- Threaded `tee_public_key`/`tee_key_epoch` into the signature and call site (already in scope in `replay_for_vault`).

This closes the crash window the PR's durability work targets — a `MkdirPublish` fsynced but never followed by the background child-IPNS publish would otherwise leave the merged parent pointing at an unresolvable child IPNS name. It also brings the mkdir replay path to parity with the file path (#19/#20/F3).

Verification: `cargo check`/`clippy -p cipherbox-fuse --features fuse` clean (only vendored `fuse_abi` noise); replay unit tests pass.

One note: my first-publish detection reuses `resolve_ipns_for_replay`, so it inherits the **cache-fallback** behavior that's already logged as a deferred TODO (`fuse-replay-ipns-strict-resolve-path-no-cache-fallback.md`). When that strict-resolve fix lands, it'll cover this new call site too — consistent with the file path. Want me to commit these, or leave them staged for you to review?

1	//! Shared journal-entry builders for the CipherBox FUSE filesystem.
2	//!
3	//! This module provides `build_upload_journal_entry` and
4	//! `build_mkdir_journal_entry` as inherent methods on [`CipherBoxFS`], so both
5	//! the fuser (macOS/Linux) and WinFsp (Windows) write paths share a single
6	//! implementation of the encrypt → ECIES-wrap → resolve-parent-IPNS →
7	//! build-`JournalEntry` steps.
8	//!
9	//! Each platform keeps its own reply/return machinery, in-memory inode
10	//! mutations, and background spawn — only the entry-build steps live here.
11	//!
12	//! ## Security invariants
13	//!
14	//! - The built `JournalEntry` references `ciphertext` only — plaintext is
15	//!   never written to the journal or to disk.  `UploadJournalResult` carries
16	//!   the plaintext transiently in memory (for the `pending_content`
17	//!   read-after-write cache); it is never persisted.
18	//! - Each key is ECIES-wrapped exactly once.  The caller must not re-wrap.
19	//! - `is_first_publish` is threaded through to the caller so the per-file
20	//!   IPNS sequence number is computed correctly (§Pitfall 4 in 45-RESEARCH.md).
21	
22	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
23	use crate::inode::{InodeKind, ROOT_INO};
24	
25	/// Result returned by [`CipherBoxFS::build_upload_journal_entry`].
26	///
27	/// Carries the built [`cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry`] and every field the
28	/// caller's inode-mutation and spawn block needs.  The `ciphertext` field
29	/// holds the AES-256-GCM encrypted file content; the `plaintext` field is
30	/// transient in-memory data for the read-after-write cache and is never
31	/// written to the journal or disk.
32	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
33	pub struct UploadJournalResult {
34	    /// The fully-built journal entry ready for `journal.put`.
35	    pub entry: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry,
36	    /// AES-256-GCM encrypted file content.
37	    pub ciphertext: Vec<u8>,
38	    /// Decrypted plaintext — used to populate `pending_content` in the
39	    /// in-memory cache so reads after write return the correct bytes without a
40	    /// network round-trip.
41	    pub plaintext: Vec<u8>,
42	    /// The `FileMetadata` struct ready for the per-file IPNS publish.
43	    pub file_meta: cipherbox_core::folder::FileMetadata,
44	    /// Raw Ed25519 private key for the per-file IPNS record (if present).
45	    /// Zeroized on drop.
46	    pub file_ipns_private_key: Option<zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>>,
47	    /// IPNS name for the per-file metadata record (if present).
48	    pub file_meta_ipns_name: Option<String>,
49	    /// Folder key used to encrypt the per-file IPNS record (if present).
50	    pub folder_key_for_file_meta: Option<Vec<u8>>,
51	    /// ECIES-hex-encoded file key, as stored in the inode and journal.
52	    pub encrypted_file_key_hex: String,
53	    /// Hex-encoded AES-GCM IV.
54	    pub iv_hex: String,
55	    /// Plaintext size in bytes (after encryption).
56	    pub file_size: u64,
57	    /// Versioning history for the new file metadata entry.
58	    pub versions_for_meta: Option<Vec<cipherbox_core::folder::VersionEntry>>,
59	    /// Inode number of the parent folder.
60	    pub parent_ino: u64,
61	    /// CID of the previous version of this file (for unpin after upload).
62	    pub old_file_cid: Option<String>,
63	    /// CIDs of versions pruned by the versioning policy (to be unpinned).
64	    pub pruned_cids: Vec<String>,
65	    /// Write generation at the time the helper was called (before any inode
66	    /// mutation by the caller).
67	    pub write_gen: u64,
68	    /// Whether this is the first publish for the per-file IPNS record.
69	    /// Passed to `publish_file_metadata` so the sequence number starts at 0.
70	    pub is_first_publish: bool,
71	}
72	
73	/// Result returned by [`CipherBoxFS::build_mkdir_journal_entry`].
74	///
75	/// Carries the built [`cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry`] and every field the
76	/// caller's spawn block needs to upload the initial folder metadata and
77	/// publish the new child + parent IPNS records.
78	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
79	pub struct MkdirJournalResult {
80	    /// The fully-built journal entry ready for `journal.put`.
81	    pub entry: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry,
82	    /// Encrypted initial folder metadata bytes ready for IPFS upload.
83	    pub json_bytes: Vec<u8>,
84	    /// Raw Ed25519 private key for the new child folder IPNS record.
85	    /// Zeroized on drop.
86	    pub ipns_private_key: zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
87	    /// TEE-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS private key (for key rotation / TEE
88	    /// republishing).  `None` if no TEE public key is configured.
89	    pub encrypted_ipns_for_tee: Option<String>,
90	    /// Current TEE key epoch (forwarded verbatim from `CipherBoxFS`).
91	    pub tee_key_epoch: Option<u32>,
92	    /// IPNS name for the newly created child folder.
93	    pub ipns_name: String,
94	    /// Parent folder metadata snapshot (with the new child already appended)
95	    /// ready for the parent IPNS publish.
96	    pub parent_metadata: cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
97	    /// Raw parent folder encryption key.
98	    pub parent_folder_key: Vec<u8>,
99	    /// Raw Ed25519 private key for the parent folder IPNS record.
100	    pub parent_ipns_key: Vec<u8>,
101	    /// IPNS name of the parent folder.
102	    pub parent_ipns_name: String,
103	    /// CID of the parent folder's previous metadata blob (for unpin on
104	    /// successful publish).
105	    pub parent_old_cid: Option<String>,
106	    /// Inode number assigned to the new child folder.
107	    pub ino: u64,
108	}

110	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
111	impl crate::CipherBoxFS {
112	    /// Build a [`JournalEntry`] for an upload (file write) without writing the
113	    /// entry to disk, mutating inodes, or spawning.
114	    ///
115	    /// Performs:
116	    /// 1. Read plaintext from the temp file handle.
117	    /// 2. AES-256-GCM encrypt + ECIES-wrap the file key.
118	    /// 3. Extract previous file metadata from the inode table.
119	    /// 4. Apply versioning policy.
120	    /// 5. Resolve `parent_folder_ipns_name` and ECIES-wrap the parent IPNS key.
121	    /// 6. ECIES-wrap the per-file IPNS key (if present).
122	    /// 7. Build and return a [`JournalOp::UploadFile`] entry.
123	    ///
124	    /// # Security
125	    ///
126	    /// Keys are wrapped exactly once.  The returned `UploadJournalResult`
127	    /// carries ciphertext only; the file key is cleared before return.
128	    pub fn build_upload_journal_entry(
129	        &self,
130	        ino: u64,
131	        handle: &crate::file_handle::OpenFileHandle,
132	        is_new_file: bool,
133	    ) -> Result<UploadJournalResult, String> {
134	        let plaintext = handle.read_all()?;
135	
136	        let mut file_key = cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_file_key();
137	        let iv = cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_iv();
138	
139	        // Zeroize the raw file key on every fallible path — `generate_file_key`
140	        // returns a plain `[u8; 32]` with no zeroize-on-drop, so an early `?`
141	        // return here would otherwise leave the key in memory.
142	        let ciphertext = match cipherbox_crypto::aes::encrypt_aes_gcm(&plaintext, &file_key, &iv) {
143	            Ok(ct) => ct,
144	            Err(e) => {
145	                cipherbox_crypto::utils::clear_bytes(&mut file_key);
146	                return Err(format!("File encryption failed: {}", e));
147	            }
148	        };
149	
150	        let wrapped_key = match cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(&file_key, &self.public_key) {
151	            Ok(wk) => wk,
152	            Err(e) => {
153	                cipherbox_crypto::utils::clear_bytes(&mut file_key);
154	                return Err(format!("Key wrapping failed: {}", e));
155	            }
156	        };
157	
158	        // Zeroize raw file key on the success path.
159	        cipherbox_crypto::utils::clear_bytes(&mut file_key);
160	
161	        let (
162	            old_file_cid,
163	            old_encrypted_key,
164	            old_iv,
165	            old_size,
166	            old_mode,
167	            existing_versions,
168	            file_ipns_private_key,
169	            file_meta_ipns_name,
170	        ) = self
171	            .inodes
172	            .get(ino)
173	            .map(|inode| match &inode.kind {
174	                InodeKind::File {
175	                    cid,
176	                    encrypted_file_key,
177	                    iv,
178	                    size,
179	                    encryption_mode,
180	                    versions,
181	                    file_ipns_private_key,
182	                    file_meta_ipns_name,
183	                    ..
184	                } => (
185	                    if cid.is_empty() {
186	                        None
187	                    } else {
188	                        Some(cid.clone())
189	                    },
190	                    encrypted_file_key.clone(),
191	                    iv.clone(),
192	                    *size,
193	                    encryption_mode.clone(),
194	                    versions.clone(),
195	                    file_ipns_private_key.clone(),
196	                    file_meta_ipns_name.clone(),
197	                ),
198	                _ => (
199	                    None,
200	                    String::new(),
201	                    String::new(),
202	                    0,
203	                    "GCM".to_string(),
204	                    None,
205	                    None,
206	                    None,
207	                ),
208	            })
209	            .unwrap_or((
210	                None,
211	                String::new(),
212	                String::new(),
213	                0,
214	                "GCM".to_string(),
215	                None,
216	                None,
217	                None,
218	            ));
219	
220	        let encrypted_file_key_hex = hex::encode(&wrapped_key);
221	        let iv_hex = hex::encode(&iv);
222	        let file_size = plaintext.len() as u64;
223	
224	        let file_name = self
225	            .inodes
226	            .get(ino)
227	            .map(|i| i.name.clone())
228	            .unwrap_or_default();
229	        let mime_type = crate::helpers::mime_from_extension(&file_name);
230	
231	        let now_ms = current_unix_ms();
232	
233	        let (new_versions, pruned_cids) = crate::helpers::apply_versioning(
234	            existing_versions,
235	            &old_file_cid,
236	            &old_encrypted_key,
237	            &old_iv,
238	            old_size,
239	            &old_mode,
240	            now_ms,
241	            self.max_versions_per_file,
242	            self.version_cooldown_ms,
243	            ino,
244	        );
245	
246	        let versions_for_meta = new_versions.as_ref().filter(|v| !v.is_empty()).cloned();
247	
248	        let (write_gen, parent_ino) = self
249	            .inodes
250	            .get(ino)
251	            .map(|i| (i.write_generation, i.parent_ino))
252	            .unwrap_or((0, ROOT_INO));
253	
254	        let folder_key_for_file_meta = self.get_folder_key(parent_ino);
255	
256	        // Resolve parent IPNS name for stable journal entry (D-02).
257	        let parent_folder_ipns_name = self
258	            .inodes
259	            .get(parent_ino)
260	            .and_then(|inode| match &inode.kind {
261	                InodeKind::Root { ipns_name, .. } => ipns_name.clone(),
262	                InodeKind::Folder { ipns_name, .. } => Some(ipns_name.clone()),
263	                _ => None,
264	            })
265	            .unwrap_or_else(|| self.root_ipns_name.clone());
266	
267	        // CR-01: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key so replay can
268	        // sign and publish the parent IPNS record at crash-recovery time.
269	        let parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal = self
270	            .inodes
271	            .get(parent_ino)
272	            .and_then(|inode| match &inode.kind {
273	                InodeKind::Root {
274	                    ipns_private_key, ..
275	                } => ipns_private_key.as_deref(),
276	                InodeKind::Folder {
277	                    ipns_private_key, ..
278	                } => ipns_private_key.as_deref(),
279	                _ => None,
280	            })
281	            .map(|raw_key| wrap_key_to_hex(raw_key, &self.public_key, "parent IPNS key"))
282	            .unwrap_or_default();
283	
284	        // Build journal entry referencing ciphertext only — no plaintext (D-05).
285	        use base64::Engine;
286	        let ciphertext_b64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&ciphertext);
287	
288	        // ECIES-wrap the per-file IPNS key exactly once (CR-01, no double-wrap).
289	        // Only journalled when there's an IPNS name to publish.
290	        let file_ipns_key_hex = if file_meta_ipns_name.is_none() {
291	            None
292	        } else {
293	            file_ipns_private_key
294	                .as_ref()
295	                .map(|k| wrap_key_to_hex(k, &self.public_key, "file IPNS key"))
296	                .or_else(|| {
297	                    self.inodes.get(ino).and_then(|i| match &i.kind {
298	                        InodeKind::File {
299	                            file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex,
300	                            ..
301	                        } => file_ipns_key_encrypted_hex.clone(),
302	                        _ => None,
303	                    })
304	                })
305	        };
306	
307	        let entry = cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry {
308	            id: generate_entry_id(),
309	            vault_root_ipns: self.root_ipns_name.clone(),
310	            op: cipherbox_sdk::JournalOp::UploadFile {
311	                ciphertext_b64,
312	                wrapped_key_hex: encrypted_file_key_hex.clone(),
313	                iv_hex: iv_hex.clone(),
314	                file_meta_ipns_name: file_meta_ipns_name.clone(),
315	                file_ipns_key_hex,
316	                parent_folder_ipns_name,
317	                parent_ipns_key_hex: parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal,
318	                filename: file_name,
319	                size: file_size,
320	                created_at_ms: now_ms,
321	            },
322	            retries: 0,
323	            status: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntryStatus::Pending,
324	        };
325	
326	        let file_meta = cipherbox_core::folder::FileMetadata {
327	            version: "v1".to_string(),
328	            cid: String::new(),
329	            file_key_encrypted: encrypted_file_key_hex.clone(),
330	            file_iv: iv_hex.clone(),
331	            size: file_size,
332	            mime_type,
333	            encryption_mode: "GCM".to_string(),
334	            created_at: now_ms,
335	            modified_at: now_ms,
336	            versions: versions_for_meta.clone(),
337	        };
338	
339	        Ok(UploadJournalResult {
340	            entry,
341	            ciphertext,
342	            plaintext,
343	            file_meta,
344	            file_ipns_private_key,
345	            file_meta_ipns_name,
346	            folder_key_for_file_meta,
347	            encrypted_file_key_hex,
348	            iv_hex,
349	            file_size,
350	            versions_for_meta,
351	            parent_ino,
352	            old_file_cid,
353	            pruned_cids,
354	            write_gen,
355	            is_first_publish: is_new_file,
356	        })
357	    }
358	
359	    /// Build a [`JournalEntry`] for a directory creation (mkdir) without
360	    /// writing the entry to disk, mutating inodes, or spawning.
361	    ///
362	    /// The caller is expected to have already:
363	    /// - Allocated `ino` via `fs.inodes.allocate_ino()`
364	    /// - Inserted the new child inode
365	    /// - Updated the parent inode's children + timestamps
366	    ///
367	    /// Performs:
368	    /// 1. Generate the initial encrypted folder metadata bytes.
369	    /// 2. Optionally ECIES-wrap the child IPNS key for the TEE.
370	    /// 3. Build the parent folder metadata snapshot via `build_folder_metadata`.
371	    /// 4. ECIES-wrap parent + child IPNS private keys (user key).
372	    /// 5. Build and return a [`JournalOp::MkdirPublish`] entry.
373	    ///
374	    /// # Security
375	    ///
376	    /// Each key is wrapped exactly once.  The TEE-wrapped key is stored
377	    /// separately from the user-ECIES-wrapped key in the result struct.
378	    pub fn build_mkdir_journal_entry(
379	        &self,
380	        parent_ino: u64,
381	        child_ino: u64,
382	        name: &str,
383	        folder_key: &[u8],
384	        ipns_name: &str,
385	        ipns_private_key: zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
386	        encrypted_folder_key_hex: &str,
387	    ) -> Result<MkdirJournalResult, String> {
388	        let metadata = cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata {
389	            version: "v2".to_string(),
390	            children: vec![],
391	        };
392	
393	        let json_bytes = crate::encrypt_metadata_to_json(&metadata, folder_key)?;
394	
395	        // TEE-wrap the child IPNS private key for republishing (if TEE configured).
396	        let encrypted_ipns_for_tee = if let Some(ref tee_key) = self.tee_public_key {
397	            let wrapped = cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, tee_key)
398	                .map_err(|e| format!("TEE key wrapping failed: {}", e))?;
399	            Some(hex::encode(&wrapped))
400	        } else {
401	            None
402	        };
403	        let tee_key_epoch = self.tee_key_epoch;
404	
405	        let (parent_metadata, parent_folder_key, parent_ipns_key, parent_ipns_name, parent_old_cid) =
406	            self.build_folder_metadata(parent_ino)?;
407	
408	        let mkdir_created_at_ms = current_unix_ms();
409	
410	        // CR-01: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key for replay signing.
411	        // An empty string (on wrap failure) parks the entry on replay rather than
412	        // persisting a degraded key silently.
413	        let parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal =
414	            wrap_key_to_hex(&parent_ipns_key, &self.public_key, "parent IPNS key");
415	
416	        // CR-03: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key (NOT TEE-wrapped) —
417	        // replay writes this into FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted, which must
418	        // be user-ECIES-wrapped.  An empty string parks the entry rather than
419	        // bricking the folder with an unusable key.
420	        let child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped =
421	            wrap_key_to_hex(&ipns_private_key, &self.public_key, "child IPNS key");
422	
423	        let entry = cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry {
424	            id: generate_entry_id(),
425	            vault_root_ipns: self.root_ipns_name.clone(),
426	            op: cipherbox_sdk::JournalOp::MkdirPublish {
427	                child_ipns_name: ipns_name.to_string(),
428	                child_folder_key_hex: encrypted_folder_key_hex.to_string(),
429	                child_ipns_key_hex: child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped,
430	                parent_folder_ipns_name: parent_ipns_name.clone(),
431	                parent_ipns_key_hex: parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal,
432	                name: name.to_string(),
433	                created_at_ms: mkdir_created_at_ms,
434	            },
435	            retries: 0,
436	            status: cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntryStatus::Pending,
437	        };
438	
439	        Ok(MkdirJournalResult {
440	            entry,
441	            json_bytes,
442	            ipns_private_key,
443	            encrypted_ipns_for_tee,
444	            tee_key_epoch,
445	            ipns_name: ipns_name.to_string(),
446	            parent_metadata,
447	            parent_folder_key,
448	            parent_ipns_key,
449	            parent_ipns_name,
450	            parent_old_cid,
451	            ino: child_ino,
452	        })
453	    }
454	}
455	
456	/// Current Unix time in milliseconds (saturating to 0 if the clock predates the epoch).
457	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
458	fn current_unix_ms() -> u64 {
459	    std::time::SystemTime::now()
460	        .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
461	        .unwrap_or_default()
462	        .as_millis() as u64
463	}
464	
465	/// Generate a fresh journal-entry id: hex-encoded 16 random bytes.
466	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
467	fn generate_entry_id() -> String {
468	    hex::encode(cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_random_bytes(16))
469	}
470	
471	/// ECIES-wrap `raw_key` for `public_key` and hex-encode it for journalling.
472	///
473	/// On wrap failure, logs a warning and returns an empty string — an empty key
474	/// field makes replay park the entry rather than persist a degraded key
475	/// (CR-01 / CR-03).
476	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
477	fn wrap_key_to_hex(raw_key: &[u8], public_key: &[u8], label: &str) -> String {
478	    cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(raw_key, public_key)
479	        .map(|w| hex::encode(&w))
480	        .unwrap_or_else(|e| {
481	            log::warn!("Failed to wrap {} for journal: {}", label, e);
482	            String::new()
483	        })
484	}
485	
486	#[cfg(test)]
487	mod tests {
488	    // Unit tests for journal_helpers live in crates/fuse/src/lib.rs (alongside
489	    // the existing characterization tests) and in crates/sdk/src/queue.rs
490	    // (journal round-trip tests).  This module intentionally has no runtime
491	    // network dependencies so there are no tests that require a live
492	    // CipherBoxFS instance here.
493	}
494

=== OpenFileHandle::new_write sig ===
    pub fn new_write(
        ino: u64,
        temp_dir: &Path,
        existing_content: Option<&[u8]>,
    ) -> Result<Self, String> {
        // Ensure temp directory exists
        fs::create_dir_all(temp_dir)
            .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to create temp dir: {}", e))?;

        // Generate unique temp file name
        let timestamp = SystemTime::now()
            .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
            .unwrap_or_default()
            .as_nanos();
        let temp_path = temp_dir.join(format!("cb-write-{}-{}", ino, timestamp));

        // Create temp file and optionally pre-populate
        let original_size = if let Some(content) = existing_content {
            fs::write(&temp_path, content)
                .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to write temp file: {}", e))?;
            content.len() as u64
        } else {
            // Create empty temp file
            fs::File::create(&temp_path)
                .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to create temp file: {}", e))?;
            0
        };

        // Restrict temp file permissions to owner-only
        #[cfg(unix)]
        {
            use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
            let _ = fs::set_permissions(&temp_path, fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600));
        }

        Ok(Self {
            ino,
            access_mode: AccessMode::ReadWrite,
            temp_path: Some(temp_path),
            dirty: false,
            cached_content: None,
            original_size,
        })
    }

    /// Write data to the temp file at the given offset. Marks the handle as dirty.
=== how a test creates write handle (file_handle.rs tests) ===
        assert!(!handle.dirty);
        assert!(handle.temp_path.is_some());
        assert_eq!(handle.original_size, 0);

        let temp_path = handle.temp_path.as_ref().unwrap();
        assert!(temp_path.exists());

        handle.cleanup();
        let _ = fs::remove_dir(&temp_dir);
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_new_write_handle_with_content() {
        let temp_dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("cipherbox-fuse-test-write-content");
        let content = b"Hello, CipherBox!";
        let handle =
            OpenFileHandle::new_write(10, &temp_dir, Some(content)).unwrap();

        assert_eq!(handle.original_size, content.len() as u64);

        let read_back = handle.read_all().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(read_back, content);

        handle.cleanup();
        let _ = fs::remove_dir(&temp_dir);
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_write_at_and_read_at() {
        let temp_dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("cipherbox-fuse-test-write-read");
        let mut handle = OpenFileHandle::new_write(
            15,
            &temp_dir,
            Some(b"Hello World"),
        )
        .unwrap();

        let written = handle.write_at(6, b"Rust!").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(written, 5);
        assert!(handle.dirty);

        let content = handle.read_all().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(&content, b"Hello Rust!");

        let partial = handle.read_at(6, 5).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(&partial, b"Rust!");
```

=== AssertSender + reply_raw test (out header layout) ===
mod test {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{FileAttr, FileType};
    use std::io::IoSlice;
    use std::sync::mpsc::{SyncSender, sync_channel};
    use std::thread;
    use std::time::{Duration, UNIX_EPOCH};
    use zerocopy::{Immutable, IntoBytes};

    #[derive(Debug, IntoBytes, Immutable)]
    #[repr(C)]
    struct Data {
        a: u8,
        b: u8,
        c: u16,
    }

    #[test]
    fn serialize_empty() {
        assert!(().as_bytes().is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn serialize_slice() {
        let data: [u8; 4] = [0x12, 0x34, 0x56, 0x78];
        assert_eq!(data.as_bytes(), [0x12, 0x34, 0x56, 0x78]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn serialize_struct() {
        let data = Data {
            a: 0x12,
            b: 0x34,
            c: 0x5678,
        };
        assert_eq!(data.as_bytes(), [0x12, 0x34, 0x78, 0x56]);
    }

    struct AssertSender {
        expected: Vec<u8>,
    }

    impl super::ReplySender for AssertSender {
        fn send(&self, data: &[IoSlice<'_>]) -> std::io::Result<()> {
            let mut v = vec![];
            for x in data {
                v.extend_from_slice(x)
            }
            assert_eq!(self.expected, v);
            Ok(())
        }

        #[cfg(feature = "abi-7-40")]
        fn open_backing(&self, _fd: BorrowedFd<'_>) -> std::io::Result<BackingId> {
            unreachable!()
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn reply_raw() {
        let data = Data {
            a: 0x12,
            b: 0x34,
            c: 0x5678,
        };
        let sender = AssertSender {
            expected: vec![
                0x14, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0xef, 0xbe, 0xad, 0xde, 0x00, 0x00,
                0x00, 0x00, 0x12, 0x34, 0x78, 0x56,
            ],
        };
        let reply: ReplyRaw = Reply::new(0xdeadbeef, sender);
        reply.send_ll(&ll::Response::new_data(data.as_bytes()));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reply_error() {
        let sender = AssertSender {
            expected: vec![
                0x10, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0xbe, 0xff, 0xff, 0xff, 0xef, 0xbe, 0xad, 0xde, 0x00, 0x00,
                0x00, 0x00,
            ],
        };
        let reply: ReplyRaw = Reply::new(0xdeadbeef, sender);
        reply.error(66);
    }

    #[test]
    fn reply_empty() {
        let sender = AssertSender {
            expected: vec![
                0x10, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0xef, 0xbe, 0xad, 0xde, 0x00, 0x00,
                0x00, 0x00,
            ],
        };
        let reply: ReplyEmpty = Reply::new(0xdeadbeef, sender);
        reply.ok();
    }

    #[test]
    fn reply_data() {
        let sender = AssertSender {
            expected: vec![
                0x14, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0xef, 0xbe, 0xad, 0xde, 0x00, 0x00,
                0x00, 0x00, 0xde, 0xad, 0xbe, 0xef,
            ],
        };
        let reply: ReplyData = Reply::new(0xdeadbeef, sender);
```

//! FUSE userspace library implementation
//!
//! This is an improved rewrite of the FUSE userspace library (lowlevel interface) to fully take
//! advantage of Rust's architecture. The only thing we rely on in the real libfuse are mount
//! and unmount calls which are needed to establish a fd to talk to the kernel driver.

//! **Note:** This crate is Unix-only. On non-Unix platforms it compiles as an empty crate

> AGENT

I've confirmed the key constraint. Here's the situation:

## Why `journal_helpers.rs` and `read_ops`/`write_ops` are fundamentally different to test

**`journal_helpers.rs` — unit-testable.** Its two builders (`build_upload_journal_entry`, `build_mkdir_journal_entry`) plus the free helpers (`wrap_key_to_hex`, `generate_entry_id`, `current_unix_ms`) are pure, synchronous, and do no network I/O. They just need a constructed `CipherBoxFS`. I can test these directly.

**`read_ops.rs` / `write_ops.rs` — not cleanly unit-testable as written.** Every handler consumes a concrete `fuser::Reply*` object (`ReplyEntry`, `ReplyAttr`, …). The only way to build one is `Reply::new(unique, sender)` where `sender: impl ReplySender` — and I verified `ReplySender` is **private to the fuser crate** (`mod reply;` is not `pub`, and only the `Reply` trait + concrete reply types are re-exported, not `ReplySender`). So `cipherbox-fuse` cannot implement a capturing sender, which means the reply objects can't be constructed in a test. On top of that, several handlers (`handle_read`, `handle_open`) do blocking network polls, so even with a sender they'd hang/EIO.

So there are only three ways to get coverage on those two files, and they're real decisions:

| Option | What it takes | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| **A. Expose `ReplySender`** | One line in our *vendored* fuser (`pub use reply::ReplySender`) + a capture-sender test helper. Then unit-test the metadata-only handlers (getattr, access, lookup, setattr, create, unlink, rmdir, rename, flush, xattr, mkdir happy-path). | Touches vendored dep; covers ~12 handlers; skips the network read/open paths |
| **B. Refactor handlers** | Split each handler into pure `-> Result<Resolved, errno>` + a thin reply shim; test the pure half. | Largest; rewrites both files |
| **C. Integration/E2E only** | Cover via a real mounted FUSE mount (the headless UAT recipe), not unit patch-coverage. | No unit tests; separate harness |

My recommendation: **do `journal_helpers.rs` now** (the original ask, clean win), and for read/write_ops go with **Option A** — it's the lowest-touch way to get genuine unit coverage, and exposing a sender trait from our own vendored fork is reasonable. I'd scope it to the metadata-only handlers and leave the network read/open paths to E2E.

Before I build the larger read/write_ops harness, one decision:

1	---
2	created: 2026-06-15
3	title: Use a strict (cache-bypassing) IPNS resolve in replay classification
4	area: fuse
5	files:
6	  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:217
7	---
8	
9	## Problem
10	
11	`resolve_ipns_for_replay` (crates/fuse/src/lib.rs ~line 217) classifies the replay IPNS
12	resolve result via `PublishCoordinator::resolve_sequence`. That coordinator method falls
13	back to a cached sequence on resolve failure: `Err(e) => match self.get_cached(name) { Some(cached) => Ok(cached), None => Err(...) }`.
14	
15	So a **transient / non-404** resolve failure with a cached sequence returns `Ok(cached)`,
16	which `resolve_ipns_for_replay` maps to `IpnsResolveOutcome::Found(cached)`. Replay then
17	continues (publishes at `cached + 1`) instead of routing through the typed
18	`IpnsResolveOutcome::Error` branch that retains the journal entry for the next mount.
19	The net effect: a network blip during replay can advance the sequence off a stale cached
20	value rather than parking the entry.
21	
22	This is **pre-existing** behavior. Phase 45 (#19) replaced the old
23	`.to_lowercase().contains("not found")` string match with the typed enum but deliberately
24	preserved the exact classification — so #19 did not change this. It was flagged by
25	CodeRabbit on PR #491 and deferred as out of scope for that no-behavior-change cleanup.
26	
27	## Solution
28	
29	TBD — add a strict resolve path so only a genuine IPNS resolve success becomes `Found`:
30	
31	- Add a cache-bypassing method on `PublishCoordinator` (e.g. `resolve_sequence_strict`)
32	  that returns `Err` on any resolve failure regardless of cache state, and call it from
33	  `resolve_ipns_for_replay`; OR
34	- Have `resolve_ipns_for_replay` call `cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns` directly
35	  and classify Found / NotFound (404) / Error itself, keeping the cache only for the
36	  sequence-advance step after a confirmed success.
37	- This is a behavior change to crash-recovery, so it needs its own tests
38	  (transient-failure-with-cache → entry retained, real 404 → first publish) and should
39	  not regress the existing replay characterization tests.
40	
41	Source: CodeRabbit review on PR #491 (thread on crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:222).
42	
```

  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-15\ntitle: Add unit test coverage for fuse journal_helpers, read_ops, and write_ops\narea: fuse\nfiles:\n  - crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs\n  - crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nThree FUSE source files currently have ~0% direct unit-test patch coverage:\n\n- `crates/fuse/src/journal_helpers.rs` — `build_upload_journal_entry`,\n  `build_mkdir_journal_entry`, and the free helpers `wrap_key_to_hex`,\n  `generate_entry_id`, `current_unix_ms`.\n- `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs` — `handle_init/destroy/lookup/getattr/open/read/`\n  `release/flush/access/getxattr/listxattr`.\n- `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs` — `handle_setattr/write/create/unlink/mkdir/rmdir/rename`.\n\nThe two are NOT equally testable, and that gates the approach:\n\n### journal_helpers.rs — unit-testable now (no blocker)\n\nThe builders are pure and synchronous (no network I/O): encrypt → ECIES-wrap →\nresolve-parent-IPNS-from-inodes → build `JournalEntry`. They need a constructed\n`CipherBoxFS` plus, for the upload path, an `OpenFileHandle` backed by a temp file\n(`OpenFileHandle::new_write(ino, temp_dir, Some(content))`). No existing test builds a\n`CipherBoxFS`, so a shared `make_test_fs()` helper is the main lift (struct has ~30\nfields incl. a `tokio::runtime::Handle`, mpsc channels, `ApiClient::new(\"http://127.0.0.1:1\")`,\n`WriteQueue::new(dir, 5)`, `PublishCoordinator::new()`). The root inode must have its\n`ipns_private_key`/`ipns_name` set (via `get_mut(ROOT_INO)`) for `build_folder_metadata`\nto succeed. Assertions are pure: ciphertext decrypts to plaintext, journal op references\nciphertext (base64) not plaintext, wrapped keys ECIES-unwrap back to originals, TEE-wrap\npresent/absent toggled by `tee_public_key`, `is_first_publish` passthrough, empty-folder\nmetadata `{version:\"v2\",children:[]}` round-trips, `status == Pending`, `retries == 0`.\n\n### read_ops.rs / write_ops.rs — blocked on a fuser limitation\n\nEvery handler consumes a concrete `fuser::Reply*` value (`ReplyEntry`, `ReplyAttr`, …).\nThe only constructor is `Reply::new(unique, sender)` where `sender: impl ReplySender`.\nIn our vendored fuser (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/vendor/fuser`, wired via the workspace\n`[patch.crates-io] fuser = { path = ... }`), `mod reply;` is private and the crate root\nre-exports only the `Reply` trait + concrete reply types — **`ReplySender` is not\nexported** (`lib_impl.rs:28`). A separate crate (`cipherbox-fuse`) therefore cannot\nimplement a capturing sender, so the reply objects cannot be constructed in a test.\nAdditionally, `handle_read`/`handle_open` do blocking network polls (up to 3s) and\nbackground prefetch, so several paths aren't pure even if a sender existed.\n\nThe reply wire format, for reference if/when this is unblocked: out-header is\n`len:u32 LE | error:i32 LE | unique:u64 LE`; `error == 0` is success, `-errno` on error\n(see fuser `reply.rs` `AssertSender` tests).\n\n## Solution\n\nDecision (2026-06-15): defer. Capture options for later:\n\n- **journal_helpers.rs (do first, standalone):** add `make_test_fs()` + builder tests.\n  No dependency on the reply blocker. This is the clean, high-value win.\n- **read_ops/write_ops — Option A (recommended if pursued):** add one line to the\n  vendored fuser (`pub use reply::ReplySender;`), write a channel-backed capture sender in\n  cipherbox-fuse test support, and unit-test the metadata-only handlers (getattr, access,\n  lookup incl. \".\"/\"..\", setattr truncate, create, unlink, rmdir, rename, flush, xattr,\n  mkdir happy-path). Leave the network read/open paths to E2E. Lowest-touch real coverage.\n- **Option B:** refactor each handler into a pure `-> Result<Resolved, errno>` core + a\n  thin reply shim; unit-test the cores. Largest change; rewrites both files.\n- **Option C:** cover via a real mounted FUSE mount (headless desktop FUSE UAT recipe) —\n  integration coverage, not unit patch-coverage.\n\nSource: follow-up from PR #491 review; testability investigation 2026-06-15.\n"
}

area: desktop-fuse

  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs

  - apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs
---

## Problem

Codecov reports phase-43 (PR #487) patch coverage at **50.1% — 638 lines missing**.
The patch status is `informational: true` (non-blocking, per `codecov.yml`), so this
did not gate the merge, but the gap is large enough to be worth closing longer term.

Breakdown of the missing lines (from the PR #487 codecov report, head `c65b797`):

| File | Patch % | Missing |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs` | 28.75% | 342 |
| `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs` | 0% | 141 |
| `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs` | 0% | 39 |
| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs` | 0% | 35 |
| `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs` | 0% | 24 |
| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs` | 0% | 18 |
| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs` | 87.38% | 14 |
| `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs` | 97.51% | 10 |
| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/tray/mod.rs` | 0% | 9 |
| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/auth.rs` | 0% | 3 |

The existing 40 fuse + 43 sdk unit tests cover the *pure* helpers (journal
serialization, queue ordering, `bridge_status`, `resolve_folder_key` bounds) but not
the FUSE callbacks or the replay orchestration, because those need a mounted FS plus a
live `PublishCoordinator`/IPNS/network. The desktop Tauri glue is exercised by the
headless UAT harness at runtime but that run is not measured by `cargo llvm-cov`.

## Solution

Three tiers, in priority order:

**Tier 1 — quick unit-test wins (no harness, ~48 lines).** Do these first.

- `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs` has four **pure free functions with zero tests**:
  `sanitize_error`, `regex_replace_paths`, `regex_replace_tokens`, `is_network_error`.
  These are log/telemetry redaction (security-relevant — they strip filesystem paths
  and tokens out of error strings) plus network-error classification. Add a
  `#[cfg(test)]` module asserting paths/tokens are scrubbed and the network-error
  matcher classifies the known cases. High value beyond the coverage number.
- `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs` (97.51%) — 10 residual error/edge branches the 17 existing
  tests miss; fill them in.
- `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs` (87.38%) — 14 residual branches in
  `bridge_status`/the daemon closure not hit by the 8 existing tests.

**Tier 2 — needs trait seams / a test harness (the bulk, ~522 lines).** This is the
real "longer term" investment: the FUSE release/write callbacks
(`read_ops.rs`/`write_ops.rs`) and the replay orchestration in `lib.rs`

===== #12 extract-shared-journal-dir-and-retries-helper (Solution section) =====
---
created: 2026-06-14T12:37:25.820Z
title: Extract shared journal-dir and max-retries helper
area: desktop-fuse
files:
  - apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs
  - apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/mod.rs
  - apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs
  - crates/sdk/src/queue.rs
---

## Problem

The journal directory path (`<data_local_dir>/cipherbox/cb-journal`, with a temp_dir
fallback) plus the magic retry count `5` are triplicated across three production sites:
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/windows/mod.rs`,
and `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs`. The code comments openly concede it
("same path the FUSE mount uses (CR-07)", "mirroring ... mod.rs").

This is a CR-07 footgun: the sync daemon and the FUSE mount MUST point at the same
directory or parked-write notifications silently break. The invariant is enforced by
three hand-copied path builders that must stay byte-identical; the moment one changes
(per-vault subdir, XDG override, different retry budget) they diverge with no error.

Surfaced by the phase-43 `/simplify` altitude reviewer; deferred from commit a1ec69f1b
because the clean fix needs a new dependency.

## Solution

Add one constructor/helper that encodes the invariant once — e.g. a
`pub fn default_journal_dir() -> PathBuf` and `pub const DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES: u32 = 5`
in `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs` — and call it from all three sites. The clean version
needs a `dirs` dependency in `crates/sdk` (the SDK currently has none); alternatively a
partial helper `journal_dir_in(base: PathBuf)` keeps `dirs` in the desktop crate while
still centralizing the `.join("cipherbox").join("cb-journal")` convention and the retry
constant. Note: `WriteQueue::default()` was intentionally removed in 43-05 to force an
explicit dir — the replacement should be this single resolver, not three open-coded copies.
```

1	---
2	created: 2026-06-14T14:26:04.000Z
3	title: Improve phase-43 rust write-durability test coverage
4	area: desktop-fuse
5	files:
6	  - crates/sdk/src/sync.rs
7	  - crates/sdk/src/queue.rs
8	  - crates/fuse/src/lib.rs
9	  - crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs
10	  - crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs
11	  - apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs
12	---
13	
14	## Problem
15	
16	Codecov reports phase-43 (PR #487) patch coverage at **50.1% — 638 lines missing**.
17	The patch status is `informational: true` (non-blocking, per `codecov.yml`), so this
18	did not gate the merge, but the gap is large enough to be worth closing longer term.
19	
20	Breakdown of the missing lines (from the PR #487 codecov report, head `c65b797`):
21	
22	| File | Patch % | Missing |
23	|---|---|---|
24	| `crates/fuse/src/lib.rs` | 28.75% | 342 |
25	| `crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs` | 0% | 141 |
26	| `crates/fuse/src/write_ops.rs` | 0% | 39 |
27	| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs` | 0% | 35 |
28	| `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs` | 0% | 24 |
29	| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sync.rs` | 0% | 18 |
30	| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs` | 87.38% | 14 |
31	| `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs` | 97.51% | 10 |
32	| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/tray/mod.rs` | 0% | 9 |
33	| `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/auth.rs` | 0% | 3 |
34	
35	The existing 40 fuse + 43 sdk unit tests cover the *pure* helpers (journal
36	serialization, queue ordering, `bridge_status`, `resolve_folder_key` bounds) but not
37	the FUSE callbacks or the replay orchestration, because those need a mounted FS plus a
38	live `PublishCoordinator`/IPNS/network. The desktop Tauri glue is exercised by the
39	headless UAT harness at runtime but that run is not measured by `cargo llvm-cov`.
40	
41	## Solution
42	
43	Three tiers, in priority order:
44	
45	**Tier 1 — quick unit-test wins (no harness, ~48 lines).** Do these first.
46	
47	- `crates/sdk/src/sync.rs` has four **pure free functions with zero tests**:
48	  `sanitize_error`, `regex_replace_paths`, `regex_replace_tokens`, `is_network_error`.
49	  These are log/telemetry redaction (security-relevant — they strip filesystem paths
50	  and tokens out of error strings) plus network-error classification. Add a
51	  `#[cfg(test)]` module asserting paths/tokens are scrubbed and the network-error
52	  matcher classifies the known cases. High value beyond the coverage number.
53	- `crates/sdk/src/queue.rs` (97.51%) — 10 residual error/edge branches the 17 existing
54	  tests miss; fill them in.
55	- `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs` (87.38%) — 14 residual branches in
56	  `bridge_status`/the daemon closure not hit by the 8 existing tests.
57	
58	**Tier 2 — needs trait seams / a test harness (the bulk, ~522 lines).** This is the
59	real "longer term" investment: the FUSE release/write callbacks
60	(`read_ops.rs`/`write_ops.rs`) and the replay orchestration in `lib.rs`
61	(`replay_for_vault`, `replay_upload_entry`, the CAS-publish tail, the full
62	`resolve_folder_key` walk) only run against a mounted FS + live coordinator. To unit
63	-test them, inject the SDK client / `PublishCoordinator` / journal behind traits so the
64	orchestration runs over mocks. This is **directly enabled by the two related deferred
65	refactors** — once the write/replay logic is a pure `CipherBoxFS` method over injected
66	deps, it is mockable in one place instead of two:
67	  - [[2026-06-14-consolidate-fuser-and-winfsp-journal-write-paths]]
68	  - [[2026-06-14-reuse-publish-file-metadata-and-cas-publish-in-replay]]
69	  Sequence Tier 2 *after* those land.
70	
71	**Tier 3 — realistically UAT-covered, do NOT chase with unit tests (~65 lines).** The
72	Tauri glue (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs`, `commands/sync.rs`,
73	`tray/mod.rs`, `commands/auth.rs`) is `#[tauri::command]` handlers + mount wiring
74	already exercised by the headless desktop UAT harness; codecov just doesn't see that
75	run. Either accept the gap or, if it must count, explore feeding the UAT run's
76	coverage into `cargo llvm-cov` — but don't write contrived unit tests for AppHandle
77	glue. `log()` this as a known, accepted gap rather than silently leaving it red.
78

  "new_string": "-test them, inject the SDK client / `PublishCoordinator` / journal behind traits so the\norchestration runs over mocks. This is **directly enabled by the two related deferred\nrefactors** — once the write/replay logic is a pure `CipherBoxFS` method over injected\ndeps, it is mockable in one place instead of two:\n  - [[2026-06-14-consolidate-fuser-and-winfsp-journal-write-paths]]\n  - [[2026-06-14-reuse-publish-file-metadata-and-cas-publish-in-replay]]\n  Sequence Tier 2 *after* those land.\n\n**Tier 2 blocker — `fuser` reply objects can't be constructed in tests (folded in from\nthe 2026-06-15 testability investigation).** Every `read_ops.rs`/`write_ops.rs` handler\nconsumes a concrete `fuser::Reply*` value. The only constructor is\n`Reply::new(unique, sender)` where `sender: impl ReplySender` — and in our vendored fuser\n(`apps/desktop/src-tauri/vendor/fuser`, wired via the workspace\n`[patch.crates-io] fuser = { path = ... }`) `mod reply;` is private and the crate root\nre-exports only the `Reply` trait + concrete reply types; **`ReplySender` is not exported**\n(`lib_impl.rs:28`). So `cipherbox-fuse` cannot implement a capturing sender, and the reply\nobjects can't be built in a unit test. Three ways forward:\n\n- **Option A (lowest-touch real coverage):** add one line to the vendored fuser\n  (`pub use reply::ReplySender;`), write a channel-backed capture sender in cipherbox-fuse\n  test support, and unit-test the metadata-only handlers (getattr, access, lookup incl.\n  \".\"/\"..\", setattr truncate, create, unlink, rmdir, rename, flush, xattr, mkdir\n  happy-path). Reply wire format: out-header is `len:u32 LE | error:i32 LE | unique:u64 LE`,\n  `error == 0` success / `-errno` on error. Leave the blocking-network `handle_read`/\n  `handle_open` paths to E2E.\n- **Option B:** the trait-seam refactor described above (decouple handlers from reply\n  emission), then unit-test the pure cores.\n- **Option C:** cover via a real mounted FUSE mount (headless desktop FUSE UAT recipe) —\n  integration coverage, not unit patch-coverage."
}

## Phases

**Phase Numbering:**

- Integer phases (1, 2, 3): Planned milestone work
- Decimal phases (2.1, 2.2): Urgent insertions (marked with INSERTED)

Decimal phases appear between their surrounding integers in numeric order.

- [x] **Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation** - Server-side Prometheus histograms and Kubo metrics scraping to establish baselines before any architectural changes (completed 2026-03-07)
- [x] **Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement** - Replace delegated-ipfs.dev with self-hosted Someguy sidecar for reliable IPNS routing, add latency histograms for resolve/publish operations (completed 2026-03-07)
- [x] **Phase 19.2: IPFS Upload Performance Optimization** - Optimize Kubo pinning path (concurrent pins, worker tuning, pin batching) to reduce upload latency (INSERTED) (completed 2026-03-23)
- [x] **Phase 20: Vault Migration** - Move rootFolderKey to IPFS vault blob v2 format, making the server store zero crypto material (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-24)
- [x] **Phase 21: BYO-IPFS Node Support** - User-configurable IPFS pinning endpoint with dual-pin strategy, Settings UI, and connection testing (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 22: Performance Baselines Completion** - Client-side timing instrumentation, end-to-end journey timing, Vitest-based load testing, and capacity documentation (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 23: Rust SDK Extraction** - Extract five Rust crates (crypto, core, api-client, fuse, sdk) mirroring the TypeScript SDK hierarchy, replace duplicated logic in desktop FUSE code, enable unit testing at same granularity as TypeScript (completed 2026-03-24)
- [x] **Phase 24: Bug Fixes & Test Infrastructure** - Fix known bugs (bin IPNS 404, device registry format error) and strengthen test infrastructure (headless load tests, vault recovery E2E, load test auth refresh) (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 25: Desktop Enhancements** - Desktop auto-update mechanism and TEE file enrollment for new files (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 26: Observability & UX Tuning** - Grafana alerting thresholds from existing baselines and timeout tuning for sub-2s UX (completed 2026-03-26)
- [x] **Phase 28: Code Hygiene & Logging** - Structured logger wrapper, replace 124 console.\* calls, fix silenced unpin failures, clean any casts, archive legacy POC (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 29: Infrastructure Hardening** - Wire up IPNS unenrollment on deletion, test login endpoint hardening, IPFS node access control (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 30: Web App Observability** - Error tracking service, error boundaries, client-side telemetry (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 31: Structural Decomposition** - Split monolithic files (useSharedNavigation, FileBrowser, folder.service) into focused modules (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 32: FUSE Async FilePointer Resolution** - Channel-based async resolution to prevent Finder disconnects from blocking FUSE thread (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 33: Windows Async FilePointer Resolution** - Port Phase 32's channel-based async FilePointer resolution to the WinFsp backend (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 34: E2E Test Expansion & Staging Baselines** - Streaming playback, media preview, batch download, and shared teardown E2E tests; BYO-IPFS load test and Faro metrics baselines on staging (completed 2026-03-29)
- [x] **Phase 35: Phala Testnet TEE Migration** - Replace staging TEE simulator with real Phala testnet CVM deployment, validate hardware-backed key derivation and IPNS republishing end-to-end (completed 2026-03-29)

## Phase Details
=== Phase 45 entry through EOF (659-708) ===
### Phase 45: Desktop FUSE write-durability cleanup

**Goal:** Rust-only hygiene refactors and added test coverage for the Phase 43/44 FUSE write journal and crash-recovery replay code. No behavior change — pay down the structural debt that accumulated while shipping durable writes, and harden the replay path with tests. Explicitly excludes the desktop-fuse data-loss bugs (mkdir-orphan, release() silent loss, stale-mount recovery), which are tracked separately as bug work.
**Requirements:** Consolidate the duplicated `fuser`/`winfsp` journal write paths; extract a shared journal-dir + max-retries helper; replace stringly-typed code in replay (empty-string journal-key sentinel → `Option<String>`, not-found string match → typed error); reduce repeated work in replay (memoize `resolve_folder_key`, reuse `publish_file_metadata` + a cas-publish helper); raise Phase-43 rust write-durability test coverage. All changes must preserve current behavior and keep crash-recovery semantics intact.
**Depends on:** Phase 44
**Plans:** 6/6 plans complete

Scope (captured todos):

- [x] #11 — Consolidate `fuser` and `winfsp` journal write paths
- [x] #12 — Extract a shared journal-dir and max-retries helper
- [x] #15 — Memoize `resolve_folder_key` during replay
- [x] #18 — Replace empty-string journal key sentinel with `Option<String>`
- [x] #19 — Replace not-found string match with a typed error in replay
- [x] #20 — Reuse `publish_file_metadata` and a cas-publish helper in replay
- [ ] #14 — Improve Phase-43 rust write-durability test coverage (Tier 1 durability/replay tests landed in Phase 45; Tier 2 read_ops/write_ops harness still open — left in pending)

Out of scope (tracked as separate bug work):

- #7 FUSE mkdir orphans the new folder when parent publish conflicts
- #8 FUSE release() reports success then can silently lose data
- #17 Recover stale FUSE mount after crash on Linux startup

Plans:
**Wave 1**

- [x] 45-01-PLAN.md — Write-durability + replay test safety net (#14)
- [x] 45-02-PLAN.md — Shared journal-dir + max-retries helper (#12)

**Wave 2** *(blocked on Wave 1 completion)*

- [x] 45-03-PLAN.md — Option<String> file-meta-ipns sentinel + serde compat (#18)

**Wave 3** *(blocked on Wave 2 completion)*

- [x] 45-04-PLAN.md — Typed IpnsResolveOutcome in replay (#19)

**Wave 4** *(blocked on Wave 3 completion)*

- [x] 45-05-PLAN.md — Reuse publish_file_metadata + memoize resolve_folder_key in replay (#20, #15)

**Wave 5** *(blocked on Wave 4 completion)*

3	title: FUSE mkdir orphans the new folder when parent publish conflicts
4	area: desktop-fuse

9	---
10	
11	## Problem
12	
13	In `handle_mkdir` the child folder's IPNS record publishes first (seq 0), then the
14	parent folder metadata CAS-publishes. On a parent-publish conflict the code only
15	warns, claiming "debounced publish will retry" — but `handle_mkdir` never calls
16	`queue_publish` / adds the parent to `mutated_folders` (`write_ops.rs:601-610`), so
17	nothing actually retries. This is the existing TODO at
18	`crates/fuse/src/platform/windows/write_ops.rs:194` (agent reports an identical
19	macOS path around `write_ops.rs:584`).
20	
21	Consequence: the child IPNS record exists remotely as an orphan, but its IPNS
22	private key and folder key live only in the parent metadata that was never
23	published. After restart the new folder is irrecoverable. A crash before the
24	spawned thread runs loses the directory entirely.
25	
26	Severity: data loss / orphaned remote state.
27	
28	## Solution
29	
30	TBD — key considerations:
